| R130 rust: `std::os::{unix,windows,wasi}::fs` had NO filesystem rule — `symlink`/`chown`/`chroot` read PURE | 2026-09-02 | **ALL FOUR ENGINE HALVES CLOSED — rust `aec6a84`, java `d8e953c`, swift `eecc458`+`29f317a`, ts `c4ae525`. PRE-EXISTING and PUBLISHED in rust, java and swift; the ts half found different defects (see below). WHAT REMAINS OPEN IS A SPEC QUESTION, NOT AN ENGINE FIX: swift answers the raw-syscall route with a DISCLOSURE and rust/java CHARGE the effect, so one program gets two gate verdicts** | **CARDINAL SIN, rust, and the shape is embarrassingly simple: `std::fs::` was the ENTIRE filesystem rule.** Everything under the platform-specific modules — `symlink`, `chown`, `lchown`, `chroot`, and the rest of `std::os::unix::fs` and its windows/wasi siblings — matched nothing and read PURE. Found while tracing the tokio difference in R123, i.e. by a question asked in passing rather than by the audit that was commissioned. **Coordinator-verified end to end, ground truth EXECUTED** — `cargo run` created a real symlink and `ls` confirms `/tmp/candor_osfs3_link -> /etc/hosts`. Against the PUBLISHED binary, in a crate containing no portable-`fs` call anywhere: **`functions: 0`, `excluded: []`, and ALL FIVE policy forms exit 0** — `deny Fs`, `deny Unknown`, `deny Fs Unknown`, `pure make_link`, `deny Fs make_link`. At the fix: `functions: 2` and all four `Fs`-bearing forms exit 1. **The control is what makes it a one-variable result:** the same write through the PORTABLE module (`std::fs::copy`) reads `['Fs']` in both binaries, so the only variable is the module path. In a crate that also uses portable `fs`, blanket `deny Fs` exits 1 — but only INCIDENTALLY, via the other call. | **The rust half is fixed with a trait-keyed DENYLIST** ([[candor-denylist-over-allowlist]]): `MetadataExt`/`PermissionsExt`/`OpenOptionsExt` and friends stay pure, `FileExt` does not — **and those exemptions are TESTED, not asserted in a comment** (§E2). A/B: **ADDED 34, REMOVED 0, nothing lost.** **THE ROW STAYS OPEN BECAUSE THE QUESTION IS FOUR-WAY AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED OF THE OTHER THREE ENGINES.** The generalisable question is not about rust: **does each engine's filesystem rule cover only the PORTABLE spelling, and what does it do with the platform-specific, POSIX or alternate-module route to the same syscall?** That is brief §F1 q3 (separate implementations that drift) crossed with §9 (do not draw the audit around its own trigger). Agents are dispatched for java, ts and swift; the rust fix is not evidence about any of them. **THE JAVA HALF CAME BACK MUCH BIGGER THAN THE QUESTION, AND THE QUESTION WAS TOO NARROW.** It is not that java's `Fs` rule covered one spelling: **THIRTEEN routes across FIVE effects** were silent, and they share one mechanism. Each was isolated in a class containing NO ordinary-spelling call, compiled and RUN so the real effect was observed — a real symlink, a real 0400 chmod, a real `user.candor` xattr, a real file trashed, 807 real OS process handles, a real child waited for, a real DNS lookup resolving `localhost` and failing on `.invalid` — then scanned: `functions: []`, `excluded: []`, **exit 0 under all five policy forms**, with the same-operation control charging and exiting 1 in the same jar. **Fs:** `java.nio.file.spi.FileSystemProvider` — **the SPI every `Files.*` method is DEFINED as a call to** — plus `FileSystem.getFileStores/getRootDirectories/newWatchService/close`, the `*AttributeView` family (chmod/chown/utimes/setxattr via a view received as a parameter), `UserPrincipalLookupService`, and `Desktop.moveToTrash`. **Exec:** `Process.onExit/inputReader/errorReader/outputWriter` and `ProcessHandle.onExit/allProcesses/children/descendants/parent/of`. **Rand:** the `RandomGenerator$Splittable/Jumpable/Streamable` sub-interfaces. **Net:** `new InetSocketAddress(String,int)`, `InetAddress.getHostName`, `Inet4/6Address` receivers, `SSLServerSocket`, `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket`, `JarURLConnection`. **Clock:** `GregorianCalendar.getInstance()`. **COORDINATOR-VERIFIED ON THE HEADLINE ROUTE, and java's failure is a STRONGER claim than rust's.** Isolated fixture, ground truth EXECUTED (`/tmp/candor_spi_link -> /etc/hosts` confirmed by `ls`): at the pre-image jar `2dd1600`, `app.Spi.viaProvider` is **PRESENT in `functions[]` with `inferred: []`** — not omitted, but **affirmatively listed as effect-free** — and all five policy forms exit 0. At `d8e953c` it is `['Fs']` and all four `Fs`-bearing forms exit 1. **In rust the unit VANISHED; in java it is reported as pure.** Same silence, but java's is a positive false statement rather than an absence, which matters for how a reader of the report is misled. **THE MECHANISM (the reusable part):** all thirteen are the classifier keying on the **static receiver type in the bytecode**, in one of three shapes — (a) a subtype-typed receiver of a modelled type, where the supertype re-classification at `Candor.handleMethodInsn` is gated on `ctx.byName.containsKey(min.owner)`, i.e. **PROJECT OWNERS ONLY, so no JDK subtype ever gets the walk**; (b) the layer BELOW a modelled facade; (c) a verb allowlist that forgot a member. Shape (a) had already been patched by hand three times (`MulticastSocket`, `SSLSocket`, Conscrypt) — **each boundary drawn around its own trigger**, which is §9 three times over. The agent widened past that by sweeping **13,324 public JDK types against the 172 owners the classifier models**; `SSLServerSocket`, `Inet4/6Address` and `JarURLConnection` came out of that sweep rather than out of anyone's list. Two §E2 hits: the `Rand` rule's comment asserted the sub-interfaces *"extend it"* (true of the type system, false of bytecode), and the `Process`/`ProcessHandle` rules were ALLOWLISTS in a file whose own `ProcessBuilder` rule argues at length that allowlists under-report silently. A/B over 395 third-party jars keyed on EVERY field: 577,547 common rows, **ADDED 107, REMOVED 0, zero losses on any field**. §E1 counters in all 14 changed branches: **11 reached** (clock.calendar 279, exec.process 111, net.sockaddr 90, fs.filesystem 61, fs.provider 57 …) — a RECALL measurement; three branches got zero corpus hits and are **safety-only over this corpus**, each resting on an executed fixture instead, and that is stated rather than blended in. Real code newly reported: httpcore5 `DefaultAddressResolver.resolve`, okhttp `Util.peerName`, jackson `InetSocketAddressSerializer`, smallrye `GetAllProcessesInfoAction`. Revert test done BY REVERTING: 6/6 positive tests go red; the 7 over-charge controls pass in both arms **and the agent explicitly does not claim they discriminate the fix**. **TWO ERRORS THE AUDIT CAUGHT IN ITSELF, both worth keeping:** its first cut FABRICATED `Fs` on `FileSystemProvider.getPath(URI)`/`getFileSystem(URI)` — four real rows in jetbrains verifier-cli — which the SPI specifies as non-I/O; carved out, pinned, A/B re-run. And `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket` first read as ALREADY COVERED because the fixture also called `ServerSocket.close()`, which is charged; re-run with the bind alone, both arms said `functions: []`. **A mixed fixture cannot answer an isolation question** — the same trap that made my own blanket-deny readings incidental three times today. **THE SWIFT HALF FOUND TWO SINS, AND THE FIRST IS THE MOST ELEGANT FAILURE OF THIS ROUND.** candor-swift already HAD a raw-syscall disclosure — R61's `native:<name>` — so `symlink` was never uncovered. **It was gated on the file importing `Darwin`/`Glibc`/`Musl`/`WinSDK`, and Foundation RE-EXPORTS the platform C library.** So the disclosure existed, was correct, and simply did not fire for the import almost every real Swift file uses. Coordinator-verified, ground truth EXECUTED in BOTH arms (each creates a real symlink, confirmed by `lstat` + `S_IFLNK` in the program's own output), isolated — no `FileManager` call anywhere — with **the import line as the only variable**: at the PUBLISHED baseline `819fac6`, `import Darwin` gives `doWork` -> `['Unknown']` + `unknownWhy: ['native:symlink']` and **`deny Unknown` exit 1**, while `import Foundation` gives **`functions: 0`, `doWork` ABSENT, and all five policy forms exit 0** — no `Unknown`, no `invisible`, no `incomplete`, a positive purity claim over an executed syscall, **for the whole R61 allowlist at once**. At HEAD both arms are identical. Fixed by removing the module gate entirely — the NAME allowlist is the only gate — plus one PROVABLE narrowing: a C function imported into Swift has no argument labels, so `remove(at: i)` cannot be libc's `remove`. **§9 half:** the original boundary had been drawn around R61's own repro, so the audit swept outward and found the `*at` twins of five listed names plus `openat`/`chroot`/`chdir`/`mkfifo`/`mknod`/`creat`/`opendir`/`readdir`/`flock`/`fsync`/`mmap`/`umask`/`waitpid`/`copyfile`/`sendfile`, the `execl*` family and the remaining `set*id` setters — **all silent under BOTH imports**. `execve`/`execvP`/`fexecve`/`posix_spawnp` were in NEITHER table and are now concretely `Exec` and ESTABLISHING, so a runtime command through them can no longer mask an `allow Exec` allowlist. **SECOND SIN (`29f317a`), and MY BRIEF'S GUESSES WERE WRONG:** I named `Data.write(to:)` and `String(contentsOfFile:)` as suspects and **both are modelled**. What was not: `NSString`/`NSDictionary`/`NSArray(contentsOfFile:)` — `NSDictionary(contentsOfFile:)` being how a decade of plist-reading Swift is written — in a classifying function whose own title is *"ONE FUNCTION, BOTH SPELLINGS"* and which knew three of the six type spellings; `NSData.write(toFile:)`, the bridged twin of the charged `Data.write(to:)`; **`URL` had no κ entry AT ALL** (`checkResourceIsReachable()` is a `stat` and the idiomatic Swift file-existence test, sibling of the always-`Fs` `FileManager.fileExists`), plus `resourceValues`/`setResourceValues`/`bookmarkData`/`resolvingSymlinksInPath`; and `OutputStream(toFileAtPath:)`/`InputStream(fileAtPath:)`, **inexpressible in `kappaFree` because it is keyed on `(name, argCount)` and only the LABEL separates a file open from `InputStream(data:)`**. A/B over 13 real packages, 11,008 common rows, wide key (13 fields): **ADDED 3, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 53**, all 56 audited against source with no sampling, branch counters confirming the corpus reaches the change (20 hits in 3 packages) — a RECALL measurement. Revert-tested by actually reverting at the parent commits: 7/9 and 5/5 rows red, the green ones named as controls. Gates all green, 1047/1047 on macOS AND under `swift:6.1` on Linux; the two macOS-SELFSKIP gates run under Docker. **A MECHANISED `swiftc -typecheck` HELPER (§E3) CAUGHT FOUR OF THE AGENT'S OWN BROKEN FIXTURES on their first Linux run** — `nil` argv for `execve`/`posix_spawnp`, and `bookmarkData`/`checkPromisedItemIsReachable`/`NSURLConnection` not existing on swift-corelibs-foundation. Those rows had been asserting things about programs that cannot exist on the platform CI runs on, which is §E3's exact failure mode caught by a tool instead of by luck. **A CROSS-ENGINE DIVERGENCE THIS EXPOSES, AND IT IS A SPEC QUESTION RATHER THAN A BUG:** swift answers the raw-syscall route with a DISCLOSURE (`Unknown` + `native:symlink`, caught by `deny Unknown`), while rust and java now CHARGE the concrete effect (`Fs`, caught by `deny Fs`). Coordinator-measured at swift HEAD: **`deny Fs doWork` still exits 0** on the symlink fixture. Both answers are honest and neither is silent, but **the same program gets a different gate verdict depending on the engine**, which is precisely what this suite exists to prevent. Tom's call, not an engine's. **THE TS HALF REFUTED MY PREMISE FOR `Fs` AND FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS IN `Net` INSTEAD — the most useful shape a brief can fail in.** candor-rust's `std::fs::`-only rule has NO ts analogue: κ's `[/^(node:)?fs(\/promises)?$/, null, 'Fs']` is keyed on the **DECLARING FILE, not the import specifier**, so every route lands on it. Measured, not assumed: **31 spellings in one tree, all `tsc --noEmit` clean, all 31 charge `Fs`** — `fs` vs `node:fs`, `fs/promises`, `fs.promises`, named/default/namespace imports, `import fs = require()`, dynamic `import()`, the Sync/callback/promise families, `createWriteStream`, the two-hop `FileHandle.write`/`writev`/`truncate`, `openSync`+`writeSync`, `cp`/`rm`/`opendir`, `realpathSync.native`, `symlink`/`chown`/`lchown`, `watch`, `statfs`, `globSync`, `new fs.WriteStream`. `original-fs` and the Deno/Bun globals are uncovered and **never claimed — a gap, not a sin**, which is the distinction the brief asked for. **DEFECT (a), SILENT UNDER-REPORT, PUBLISHED — and it is an exfiltration primitive.** `@types/node/web-globals/fetch.d.ts` re-exports `WebSocket`/`EventSource` from the **`undici-types` PACKAGE**, so `declIsNodeTypes` is false, the node-core floor is never consulted, and κ had no rule. **This is the FOURTH instance of the R109/R110/R111 tsconfig-split class.** Coordinator-verified, isolated file, `tsc` clean, one variable: at the pre-image, `dial` (`new WebSocket(u)`), `exfil` (`w.send(secret)`), `stream` (`new EventSource(u)`) and `viaSubclass` (`class D extends WebSocket { super(u) }`) ALL read **`inferred: []`** and **every one of `deny Net`, `deny Unknown`, `deny Net Unknown`, `pure`, and the scoped deny exits 0**. At HEAD all four carry `['Net']` and the four `Net`-bearing forms exit 1. **A DETAIL I MEASURED DIFFERENTLY FROM THE AGENT, and it matters:** the agent reported two rows carrying no `invisible`; in my fixture **all four carry `invisible: ['undici-types']`**. So a breadcrumb naming the package IS emitted — **and no policy form reads it, `deny Net Unknown` included.** That is arguably worse than plain silence to reason about: the report contains a true statement that the scan could not see something, and **there is no gate that turns that statement into a verdict.** The gate consequence is identical to silence. `NODE_CORE_REVIEWED` asserts *"`WebSocket` is deliberately absent — it CONNECTS"* and **the name never reached that list** — §E2, a comment asserting the safety of its own diff. Ground truth EXECUTED by the agent against a real 127.0.0.1 listener: the ctor fires `upgrade` once, `ws.send('SECRET-EXFIL')` delivers one frame carrying that exact unmasked payload, `new EventSource(…/sse)` issues one HTTP request. **The `extends`/`super` spelling came from REAL CODE, not enumeration** — crossws `src/websocket/bun.ts` — found by re-scanning a corpus under a node-style tsconfig **after the first cut stopped at its own trigger** (§9, caught by the agent on itself). **DEFECT (b), FABRICATION, PUBLISHED.** ⟨0.29⟩ fixed the payload-as-locator class for Exec/Fs/Db and, in Net, fixed only the POSITION and dgram's `send`. So `export function w(s: net.Socket) { s.write('api.example.com'); }` published `hosts: ['api.example.com']` with **no `incomplete`**, and **`allow Net in src api.example.com` answered `policy ✓` exit 0** over a socket whose destination the scan never saw. `netEstablishing` already excludes use-calls and says so — it governed the `incomplete` branch and **the capture branch never asked it**. A/B over 8 corpora, 2,561 common rows, 16-field key: ADDED 9 REMOVED 2 CHANGED(wide) 102 CHANGED(narrow) 40, **zero effects lost**, both removals ground-truthed from source as genuinely pure `new Headers(...)` builders. **The six library corpora reach the `undici-types` arm ZERO times** — all leave `lib` unset or name `dom` — so over those it is SAFETY-ONLY, and the recall evidence is the real crossws source under a node tsconfig, stated rather than blended. Revert-tested three ways (34 / 28 / 4 rows red respectively), and **one drafted row that could not discriminate either way was REPLACED rather than kept as fake coverage.** **JAVA FOLLOW-UP CLOSED — candor-java `81f4ceb`. Three fabrications `d8e953c` introduced, plus a published clock sin it sat beside; all four reproduced BEFORE any fix.** **B2 (`FileSystemProvider.getFileAttributeView` charged `Fs` doing no I/O) fixed by sweeping the SPI by SPECIFICATION rather than by corpus** — the discriminator is that every I/O member declares `throws IOException` and exactly five do not. It stays a DENYLIST because two counterexamples exist and are named in the comment (`installedProviders()`, JDK 20's `exists()`), which is the right reason to keep one. **B3 (SSLServerSocket option setters, `getOption`/`setOption`/`supportedOptions` charged `Net`) fixed AND the arms converged**: five hand-copied `method.equals` chains became three predicates defined once and shared by all six socket owners. The agent's reasoning is the §G point exactly — *fixing the TLS acceptor alone would have converged one pair of arms by splitting another* — so it fixed the family. Wire boundary unchanged, pinned by a 17-member control. **B4, the pre-existing published clock sin, fixed and coordinator-verified**: `new GregorianCalendar(TimeZone/Locale/both)` reads the wall clock and was certified pure. Executed — the TZ ctor returns values 1.1s apart while `(y,m,d)` returns identical ones — and measured at the pre-image jar `viaTz` is **ABSENT**, at the fix `['Clock']`, with the fixed-date control staying ABSENT in both (no over-charge). **Arity was never the discriminator; argument KIND is** — which is what the old ARITY-PRECISE comment got wrong. **B1 WAS DELIBERATELY NOT NARROWED, AND THE REFUSAL IS THE BEST RESULT OF THE FOUR.** A wrapper over a real child reaches its capability **through that supertype walk and nothing else** — `calls: None`, because the JDK body is never scanned — so narrowing would have traded a loud over-charge for a SILENT UNDER-REPORT. Measured, not argued. The false clause is rewritten to state its assumption (§E2) and both directions are pinned. §9 sweep: the same shape reaches `FileSystemProvider` and `RandomGenerator` (pinned); `ProcessHandle` is unaffected. **Premise correction:** B1's fabrication class is NOT new — `destroyForcibly`, also inherited-concrete, was already charged at `2dd1600`; `d8e953c` widened it from one member to eleven. **The instrument was calibrated before it was trusted:** the `2dd1600 -> d8e953c` A/B arm reproduces the earlier fix's numbers EXACTLY (577,547 common, ADDED 107, REMOVED 0). Then `d8e953c -> HEAD`: ADDED 254, REMOVED 118, CHANGED(wide) 1,839 over 8 fields, two mechanisms (+117 Clock gains, -118 from the narrowings). **All 146 lost rows audited in FULL from `javap` — 102 direct-clean, 44 transitive, 0 suspicious, 0 unexplained** — and all 37 direct Clock gains confirmed against their own bytecode. **The agent's own audit tool had a bug it caught and fixed:** its `javap` regex missed the quoted `"<init>"` spelling, so the first Clock pass reported 0 of 37 confirmed. Both audits re-run. Gates: `test` 953/0, `smoke.sh`, `soundness/run.sh 40`, `reentrancy.sh`, `verify-local.sh` all OK. **`ci/self-gate.sh` RUN BY HAND — exit 0, "no violations"** — because `gate-run.sh` structurally cannot see inside a `run: |` block, and this is the gate from CLAUDE.md's four-red-commits story. `nativeCompile` UNRUN and **verified to fail identically at the merge-base** rather than assumed. |

Closed as a documentation note — the row's question was a spec-design decision, not a defect. What was wrong was reading that as a defect. Written in lowercase deliberately: it documents the existing design and adds no requirement, so the MUST ledger is unchanged at 529 statements. Counting is SYMMETRIC, so a macro invoked inside the declaring block is still promoted (`macro_rules!` is unhygienic for items and types). That is §E1 applied against the agent's own result. Revert test done BY REVERTING: `r139_a_local_macro_template_counts_toward_the_body_shadow` fails (`left: []`, `right: ['Exec']`). Each test was separately proven able to fail, and the commit records that only the `invoked twice` arm catches a seen-set recursion guard while the monotonicity test does not — stated rather than glossed. **§E2:** `decls.rs:419-424`'s false *"every position an identifier can occupy has to be reached"* is replaced with a checkable property — *every ident the `CallCollector` resolves through the `uses` map while walking this body* — bounded by the fact that `local_macros` is the only `CallCollector` field carrying token-level syntax from outside the body. **[Label corrected 2026-09-03 by the 0.35.0 release panel: `3cf055d` is NOT the published 0.34.0 — the v0.34.0 tag is `736fa64`, six commits earlier; where this row says 'published 3cf055d' read 'post-tag 3cf055d'. Cause on this machine: `javac` on PATH is 17 while the Gradle toolchain is 21, so `Thread.ofVirtual()` does not compile. Reproduces identically before and after R163. Detection worked; aggregation discarded it. The decision it needs — hard-fail on a missing toolchain, or SELFSKIP loudly — is Tom's, which is why two agents in a row left it alone. **RULED by Tom 2026-09-03: a missing toolchain makes `soundness/dynamic/corpus.sh` SELFSKIP loudly (exit 3, the family convention), never CLEAN and never FAIL. Now `compile_one` captures each rc and its first error line, the missing entry is recorded, and `main` checks a genuine finding FIRST (always exit 1) before reporting SELFSKIP. **All three arms executed, not reasoned about:** missing prerequisite -> exit 3 with `RESULT: SELFSKIP - 1 corpus entry could not be analysed (prerequisite missing): async-netfs (javac exit 1 ...)`; the same corpus with that entry excluded -> exit 0 CLEAN; a real finding (a test hook stripping `Fs` from `hof-io`'s report, caught by the real JFR oracle) -> exit 1, NOT 3. Wiring the script into an auto-run gate is a separate change, in a repo that agent did not own. |

Caught by grepping the new block for backticks in executable lines. They are bounded — a present-but-unbuildable swift now dies at this guard before reaching them — so they are reachable only through `part.sh` filtered slices, which is where the mislabels remain. |

The docs should change, and the durable fix is a conformance-side check of each `AGENTS.md` and each embedded contract **against the SPEC vocabulary** — otherwise the next tier-1 additive rung repeats this exactly. **THE DURABLE FIX IS IN:** `scripts/check_agents_vocabulary.py` derives the vocabulary MECHANICALLY from SPEC.md §1's effect table (11 declarable names; `Unknown` excluded because the SPEC defines it as a visibility marker, not a declarable effect) and checks eight doc copies plus four embedded `--agents` contracts (UNRUN, never green, when a binary is absent). Wired into `conformance.yml`'s `agents-doc-drift` job — **which previously checked out candor-spec alone and would have checked nothing but its own file**; the job now checks out all five siblings (source only), so the gate enforces. §E3: run BEFORE any fix it was red on all 8 docs and all 4 contracts for `Llm` — a gate proven able to fail before it was trusted. **AND THE PREMISE IN MY BRIEF WAS WRONG:** I expected it to stay red only on rust and java. The agent fixed exactly `Llm` as instructed and REPORTED the rest rather than widening its diff — the right call, and the reason these are on the record. | R157 rust: `coverage-gate-refresh` has NEVER been green — its "stable" step runs nightly and links with a linker it never installs | 2026-09-02 | **OPEN — INSTRUMENT defect, present since the workflow was created (`2ab04a4`, 2026-08-27). Coordinator-diagnosed from CI's own log; local reproduction FAILED and that is stated** | **A weekly drift check that has never once run.** The job exists to re-fetch ~74 calibrated crates from live crates.io and diff the coverage manifest — the staleness answer the fast gate structurally cannot give. `gh run list` shows exactly TWO runs ever: today's first scheduled run at `3cf055d` and my `workflow_dispatch` at `c4571a9`, **both red at the same step, before any fetch or diff.** **Cause, from the runner's own log:** `info: note that the toolchain 'nightly-2026-06-14-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu' is currently in use (overridden by …/rust-toolchain)`. The step is named *"Build the stable scanner"* and runs bare `cargo build --release -p candor-scan`; `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable` installs stable, but the repo's `rust-toolchain` file overrides any bare `cargo`, so it builds with the pinned NIGHTLY. `.cargo/config.toml` then forces `rustflags = ["-C", "linker=dylint-link"]` for every target, the job never installs `dylint-link`, and every dependency's build script dies at link: `error: linker \`dylint-link\` not found`. **The green sibling in `ci.yml:344` already documents both traps and escapes both** — `RUSTFLAGS= cargo +stable test …`, with a comment explaining that set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS` overrides the workspace linker and that `+stable` is deliberate. The failing workflow was written 17 days later without either. §F1 q3: one question — *how do we build on stable in CI* — answered twice, and the second copy never checked against the first. | Not fixed — the fix is one line (`coverage-gate-refresh.yml:37` → `RUSTFLAGS= cargo +stable build --release -p candor-scan`, matching `ci.yml:344`) and is held only because another agent owns candor-rust at the time of writing. Evidence that the fix works on the runner is the sibling job, green on every push. **Reproduction limit, stated rather than hidden:** I could NOT reproduce the failure on macOS. With `dylint-link` removed from PATH and an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, both `cargo +stable` and `cargo +nightly-2026-06-14` passed `-C linker=dylint-link` to rustc (44 invocations, verified with `-v`) and **succeeded anyway; a fake `dylint-link` placed as the only one on PATH was invoked 0 times, even by a direct `rustc -C linker=dylint-link main.rs`.** I do not know why Apple-target rustc accepts and ignores that flag here, and I am not guessing. The diagnosis rests on the Linux log, not on a local repro. **Two instrument lessons worth more than the fix:** (1) my first three reproduction arms returned **exit 127** — I had stripped `~/.cargo/bin` to hide the linker and hid `rustup` with it. A dead instrument, read as a result, twice in one day. (2) I had earlier reported *"no `rust-toolchain.toml`"* as evidence — the file is `rust-toolchain`, no extension; a `cat` of the wrong name is not a measurement of absence. **PROVEN ON THE RUNNER, THEN THE SAME DEFECT ONE LAYER DOWN.** With the one-line fix (`4ba1350`) the *"Build the stable scanner"* step went **green for the first time in the job's existence**, and so did the crates.io fetch — the diagnosis that would not reproduce on macOS was correct on Linux. The failure then moved to *"Regenerate covered.tsv / open.tsv"*: `generate.py` shells out to a **bare `cargo run`** for its `classify_check` helper, and that subprocess inherited the nightly pin and the dylint linker exactly as the step's own cargo had — `linker \`dylint-link\` not found` again, **after 74 crates were fetched and 21,999 candidate entries collected.** The diff and issue steps were correctly skipped, so this was still not drift being reported. A per-step `+stable` cannot reach a cargo that Python spawns, so the second fix (`candor-rust`, follow-up commit) sets the decision ONCE at the job level: `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN: stable` and a set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS`. **Both halves measured, not assumed:** bare `cargo --version` inside the repo prints `1.98.0-nightly` and with the variable prints `1.98.0` stable — so the env var beats the toolchain file; and set-but-empty `RUSTFLAGS` overriding the workspace linker is the fact `ci.yml:344` already relies on. **And forcing stable is the RIGHT mechanism rather than a workaround**, checked before choosing it over the alternative of installing `dylint-link` the way `ci.yml`'s ubuntu lane does: `classify_check` depends only on `candor-classify` and serde — no `#![feature]`, no `rustc_private`, no dylint — so it is a stable crate that was being built on nightly for no reason. Installing the linker would have satisfied the error while leaving the wrong toolchain in place. Re-dispatched; **the job's first-ever complete green, or its first-ever real drift finding, is the outcome to check next** — either would be a first. |

| R160 rust: a public forwarder that calls a sibling via `Self::` is ABSENT from the report | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `bb4851b`. PUBLISHED cardinal sin; `Self::` static-call resolution had NEVER existed in any scanner. Coordinator-verified pre- and post-fix on the fixture and on rusqlite 0.40.2** | **CARDINAL SIN, rust, and it is rusqlite's front door.** `impl Conn { pub fn open(p) { Self::open_with_flags(p, 0) } pub fn open_with_flags(p, f) { std::fs::read_to_string(p)… } }` — `open_with_flags` reports `['Fs']`; **`open` is ABSENT** from `functions[]` on the manifest-era scanner (`79546f3`), the published `3cf055d`, and HEAD. **The control differs in exactly one token:** `open2` written as `Conn::open_with_flags(p, 0)` instead of `Self::…` reports `['Fs']`. So a `Self::`-qualified static call to a sibling is not resolved as a call edge, the forwarder reaches nothing, and the *mint no unit unless it carries or reaches an effect* filter omits it. Real instance: **`rusqlite::Connection::open`** (and `open_in_memory`) — absent on rusqlite 0.40.2 under all three scanners while `Connection::open_with_flags` reads `['Db','Unknown']`. The 08-28 coverage manifest still records `rusqlite::Connection::open` as covered, so an even earlier scanner reported it; when it was lost is not yet bisected. | Not fixed. **Gate consequence is the R79/R85 shape**: the callee is reported so a blanket `deny Db` fires incidentally, but `deny Db <caller>`, `pure <caller>`, `path`, `callers` and every question about WHO reaches the effect are blind for the function users actually call. **Fix direction:** resolve `Self::name(...)` in a static-call position exactly as `<EnclosingType>::name(...)` — one authority, not a second arm — and sweep `Self::` in every position the existing type-qualified resolution handles (assoc fn, assoc const, trait static, `<Self as Trait>::`). **FIXED, and the row's one dating claim corrected.** I wrote that an earlier scanner must have reported `rusqlite::Connection::open` because the 08-28 manifest lists it covered. **Wrong:** the oldest buildable scanner (0.3.5, `2952f78`, 2026-06-11) is already ABSENT on the fixture and on rusqlite, and `git log -S` over the whole history finds no commit that ever contained a `Self`-headed resolution arm. **`Self::` resolution never existed**; the manifest row is R162's stale-manifest story, not a scanner regression. **Fix (§G, one authority):** `scan_items` binds `Self` in the ordinary `use` map for the length of one impl block (and to the trait inside a trait default body, as that arm already does for `self`); `lang::expand` — the single place a written path becomes a resolved one — then answers every position, so there is no second arm. The value is `expand(<type>, uses)`, not the bare leaf, so `use inner::Far; impl Reach for Far` resolves `Self::touch` to `inner::Far::touch` exactly as `Far::touch()` does. **Position sweep, built and run:** assoc fn, `map(Self::f)`, nested closure, 3-deep chain, trait-default `Self::helper()`, `Self::new()` in a generic impl, impl-for-imported-type — all fixed. `Self::CONST` and `Self::Variant(..)` expand but match no fn unit, so nothing is charged (parity with `K::NAME`/`State::Open(n)`); **a leaf fallback was deliberately NOT used** because it would have linked `Self::NAME` to an effectful free fn `NAME` beside it. **Still under-reporting, stated:** `<Self as Trait>::m` with no default body (its `<Type as Trait>::m` control fails identically, so not a `Self` issue), `Self::required()` in a trait default, `impl Trait for &[u8]`. **Corpus A/B, 1489 crates, HEAD vs HEAD+fix, every field:** ADDED 2621, **REMOVED 2**, CHANGED 9227 — `calls` +7982, `invisible` +2969, `direct` −2094, `unknownWhy` −2094, **`inferred` +929 pure gain, 0 loss, 0 mixed**, `unresolved` +736, `incomplete` +88. §E1: `SELFALIAS` hit **231,409 times across 1037 of 1490 crates, 0 in the PRE arm**. Both removals audited from source — `crossbeam_epoch::Owned::with_tag` at two versions, whose `self.into_usize()` calls `mem::forget(self)`, so no drop runs: POST correct, PRE was an over-charge. **Near-miss reported, not hidden:** that row is right for a shaky reason — the escape gate is LEAF-keyed and suppresses the drop charge for a consumed by-value `self` whenever the function returns a fresh value of the same type leaf, forget or no forget; on a synthetic `via_self` its `G::mk()` control is absent in BOTH arms, so the fix brings `Self::` into parity with an existing silence rather than creating one (R168). The `unknownWhy` −2094 cluster is js-sys, where the reason moves one hop to the resolved callee; `deny Unknown[native]` over js-sys returns 180 violations in both arms. **Gate consequence:** blanket `deny Fs` fires in both arms via the callee; **`pure Conn::open` and `deny Fs Conn::open` go exit 0 → 1 on the lone-forwarder fixture `scratchpad/rsfwd-iso`** (PRE: the rule binds no unit; HEAD: 1 violation). The mechanism deletes the graph edge to the `Drop` unit with no residual marker, which is what defeats scoped policies, `path`, `gains` and `fix-gate` while blanket `deny Db` still fires via the independently-listed `Type::drop` units. **Sound direction per the family's own rule:** carve out PROVEN-safe escapes only; an escape through a closure or through a free-fn constructor is not proven and should either charge or leave a tagged edge for the transitive walk to resolve. **THE OPEN HALF RESOLVED, and the row's framing fell with it.** Nine callback-parameter spellings, each an executing fixture (the callback really writes a file), scanned on the manifest-era, published and HEAD binaries: `fn(T)->U`, `F: Fn`, `impl Fn`, `&dyn Fn`, `Box<dyn Fn>`, a fn pointer in a struct field, and `unsafe extern "C" fn` all read `Unknown` + `callback:unresolved call` on all three — correct. **`f: Alias` where `pub type Alias = fn(T) -> U`, and `f: Option<fn(T) -> U>`, are ABSENT from `functions[]` on all three** — identical bytes pre- and post-`7af62f1`, and reproducible on a fixture with no `Drop` type at all, so orthogonal to the bisected commit. **Why `init_auto_extension` ever showed `['Db']`:** never from its own `ax: AutoExtension` callback — `is_callable_type` has never fired on that alias in any binary. It **inherited** `from_handle`'s over-charge transitively (`from_handle` is in its `calls` on all three). When `7af62f1` correctly removed that fabrication, the inherited charge vanished and exposed the pre-existing alias gap. **A correct verdict produced by an incorrect mechanism, unmasked by removing the over-charge — R147's shape exactly.** Two unrelated defects that flipped in one commit. **Mechanism, line-pinned:** `is_callable_type` (`crates/candor-scan/src/lang.rs:311-350`) matches `Type::BareFn` (:313), `Fn*` trait leaves (:318), and for a bare `Type::Path` only the wrappers `Box`/`Rc`/`Arc`/`Symbol` (:337-344); any other named path — an alias, or `Option<…>` — falls through to `false` (:339/:346-348). With other recorded calls present the loss surfaces as an explicit `[]` with no `unknownWhy`; when the callback is the function's ONLY call, the function is pruned from the report — an affirmative purity claim under SPEC §2 rule 3, the worse symptom. **Fix shape (not implemented; rust editor is busy):** (1) make `is_callable_type` alias-aware; (2) let the alias recorder capture a `BareFn` RHS (and any RHS carrying an `Fn*` leaf); (3) apply `unwrap_result_option` at every parameter-position call site as `record_return` already does. **Not established:** nested spellings (`&Option<fn>`, `Box<Alias>`, alias-to-`Box<dyn Fn>`, double aliases) and the three further `is_callable_type` sites at `collector.rs:1460/1491/1710`. **Instrument note from the agent, worth keeping:** the `HEAD` binary it used was built from a DIRTY working tree (the R160 agent mid-edit in `decls.rs`/`lang.rs`) — results were byte-identical to the two clean binaries for everything scanned, so its conclusions stand, but *`target/release/candor-scan` is not HEAD while an agent is editing that repo*. **FIXED.** `is_callable_type` gained a crate-wide `callable_aliases` index (collected in the same `syn::Item::Type` arm as `prim_aliases`) and now applies `unwrap_result_option`, which only `record_return` had ever done and only to a RETURN type; `elem_trait_leaves`' payload resolver gained a synthetic `"Fn"` leaf for a bare fn pointer — which is why `Option<Box<dyn Fn>>` always worked and `Option<fn>` never did. Executed 10-spelling fixture: `Alias`, `Box<dyn Fn>`-alias, `&Alias`, `Option<fn>` if-let and `.map` all ABSENT → `['Unknown']` + `callback:unresolved call`; purity controls unchanged; 2 tests red on revert. ## ⚠ OWED TO TOM — a gate-semantics question raised by R105's fix (2026-09-02)

R105 replaced "pick the `#[cfg]` arm that happens to be last in the file" with an honest
`Unknown` + `ambiguous:` when the arms classify differently. That is unambiguously more truthful. **But it
changes what a bare `deny <E>` does, and the change deserves a ruling rather than a default.**

Measured on two crates identical but for arm order, one arm `Fs` and one `Env`:

| policy | pre-fix (arm A) | pre-fix (arm B) | post-fix (both) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deny Fs` | 1 | 0 | **0** |
| `deny Unknown` | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| `deny Fs Unknown` | 0 | 0 | **1** |

Post-fix the two orders agree under every form — that is the point, and it is right. The question is the
first row. **Pre-fix, `deny Fs` fired for roughly half the affected crates by coin-flip. Post-fix it never
fires**, because the honest answer is `Unknown` and a bare `deny <E>` does not act on `Unknown`.

**The engine now KNOWS `Fs` is one of exactly two possibilities and does not fail closed on it.** That is
defensible — `deny Unknown` is the documented upgrade and it does fire — but it is a different posture
from the rest of the family, where an effect the engine can name is denied. The alternative is: **a
`deny <E>` fires when `<E>` appears among the arms of an `ambiguous:` disclosure.**

This is a **cross-engine SPEC question**, not a rust one — java/ts/swift have not been measured for the
same shape. No engine or spec text was changed. **Tom's call.**

## THE REUSABLE QUESTION FROM THE R109–R115 VEIN (2026-09-02)

Seven rows came out of one week-old-looking gap, and the question that produced five of them is not about
web globals at all:

> **Where does the mechanism that answers a question actually FIRE, and does the syntax that ASKS the
> question ever reach it?**

**"Not charged" and "not reachable by this mechanism" are different facts, and only the second one tells
you where to look next.** R111 wrote that distinction down as an aside — that `timeOrigin` and
`nodeTiming` are property accesses while the classify site fires only on calls — and that aside found
R113, R114 and R115.

Three corollaries, each measured rather than reasoned:

1. **A curated table is bounded by its call site, not by its contents.** Both of candor-ts's web-global
   tables are exhaustive-looking and sit under `isCallExpression || isNewExpression`. No amount of adding
   members reaches a property.
2. **Agreement between two resolution paths is worthless as a signal** (R111). R113 is the fourth instance
   of both-arms-agreeing-while-both-are-wrong, and it was sitting inside a comment that used the agreement
   as the reason to dismiss it.
3. **The same site can fail in both directions at once.** R113/R114 are silent under-reports; R115 is a
   fabrication that fails `deny Fs` on a function that performs nothing — same property machinery, opposite
   errors. Auditing only the miss direction would have found three of four.

**And the class turned out BOUNDED, which is the actionable part.** A table keyed on resolved-declaration
identity fires on **30 of 28,730 property accesses (0.104%)** across six real repos — as narrow as the
call-arm branches it would sit beside. The naive fear ("charging property reads would be catastrophic") is
true only of the shape nobody proposed: 52% of those accesses fall in the no-symbol bucket. **Treat 0.104%
as a floor** — that corpus is TS libraries, and `document.cookie` appears once in it, `localStorage.x = v`
zero times; a browser-application corpus would price it differently and was not built.
